## Semantic Chunks
### Section: Introduction
- **Level**: `intro`
- **Hash**: `f77c27d26c05002385180327b6350cddf9e346df744f337da9af420baba2977a`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/how-can-teachers-detect-ai-assisted-plagiarism` (Similarity: 0.9402)

```text
Checking an assignment for both AI generation and traditional plagiarism is no longer optional—it is essential for modern academic integrity.

Because traditional plagiarism checkers search only for exact text matches on published websites, they are completely blind to freshly generated AI prose from tools like ChatGPT. Conversely, an AI detector analyzes statistical language patterns but cannot confirm whether a student copied verbatim paragraphs from a peer's unindexed document. Evaluating student submissions requires a unified, multi-signal approach that checks web similarity, AI probability, and document creation history simultaneously.

Below is a step-by-step educator guide on how to inspect assignments for both threats efficiently and accurately within your standard grading workflow.

Checkmark Plagiarism streamlines this dual verification by uniting plagiarism detection with AI detection , essay writing playback , static AI detection, autograding , and integrations with Canvas and Google Classroom .
```

### Section: The 4 Integrity Quadrants: Interpreting Dual Results
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `1033efc22f9c284be3a768baa35cd0144329e60bf0a19588424e6248a9857788`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/how-can-ap-seminar-teachers-use-quote-anchored-rubric-feedback-to-accelerate-first-draft-research-scoring` (Similarity: 0.8949)

```text
1. High AI / Low Plagiarism

The text was generated by a language model like ChatGPT. It contains novel phrasing that avoids web matches but exhibits AI predictability.

2. High Plagiarism / Low AI

Traditional copy-paste cheating: the student copied directly from Wikipedia, a journal, or a website without using generative AI.

3. High AI / High Plagiarism

AI-assisted patchwriting: the student pasted stolen text into an AI tool, which lightly rephrased it while retaining exact matching fragments.

4. Low AI / Low Plagiarism

Authentic student writing: the paper is free from database matches and exhibits natural human sentence rhythm and burstiness.
```

### Section: A 5-Point Educator Checklist for Grading Days
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `2da6bfdf228e528c1e6fa15477fb30f81720e3c7fea59a79b358b77f4daad036`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/whats-the-difference-between-ai-writing-plagiarism-and-paraphrasing` (Similarity: 0.9167)

```text
Dual Integrity Checklist for Instructors:

1. Enable dual AI and plagiarism scanning on all major writing assignments. 
 2. Check whether flagged text represents legitimate properly cited quotations. 
 3. Inspect writing playback logs for wholesale paste events and active drafting duration. 
 4. Audit 2–3 bibliography citations in academic databases for hallucinations. 
 5. Hold a brief conference if multiple independent signals point to unauthorized assistance.
```
